Without proper medical guidance, Joshua experienced precipitated withdrawal and took the full supply over several days, hoping it would reset his receptors.

He disclosed the situation directly to Dee-Dee.

Instead of following up with Joshua, Dee-Dee began asking other homeless individuals whether he had relapsed and later confronted Janina with: "Be honest." After this, Dee-Dee stopped responding entirely to our messages-more than 100 texts were sent with no reply.

Several months later, she reappeared briefly but showed no interest in our case.

When May passed, she told us she had actually said September.

That was the last time we heard from her.

A Pattern of False Hope What follows is a detailed record of how New Hope C.O.R.P.S. subjected us to a pattern of false hope, deliberate delays, and eventual betrayal.

False Hope Instance #1 - Dee-Dee's Promises, Disappearance, and Final Deflection In December 2021, we were approached by Dee-Dee, a staff member from New Hope, who expressed strong enthusiasm about our situation.

In January 2022, Janina Miranda was formally assessed.

Dee-Dee encouraged us to do the assessments separately, claiming it would increase our chances.

Janina even created a list of over 20 available rental listings and gave them to Dee-Dee to pursue.

Dee-Dee also provided Suboxone through New Hope's outreach to help Joshua detox.

Unfortunately, due to the lack of medical guidance, Joshua went into precipitated withdrawal and took the entire supply over a few days, believing it would reset his receptors.

He was honest about what had happened and told Dee-Dee directly.

Instead of addressing the situation supportively, Dee-Dee never asked Joshua directly about his health-instead, she violated his privacy by asking other unhoused individuals if he had relapsed, and then confronted Janina with the leading statement: "Be honest." After that, she ghosted us for several months, ignoring over 100 texts.

Then, one day, she reappeared without warning, clearly disinterested in our situation.

She said New Hope's budget had run out, and they'd get new funding in May.

But when May came and went, and we reached out to her, she claimed she had told us September instead.

That was the last time we ever heard from Dee-Dee.

Her abandonment, privacy violations, and vague shifting promises destroyed months of momentum.

Her departure, however, did not mean her influence disappeared.

Her malicious decisions behind the scenes would go on to harm us for months to come.

False Hope Instance #2 - Adam's Apology and Disappearance Several months after Dee-Dee's final deflection, a man named Adam from New Hope unexpectedly appeared.

He told us he had reviewed our file and seemed remorseful-not direct in his apology, but clearly regretful.

He asked us to give him the chance to "fix what Dee-Dee did." We were stunned.

After everything, the idea that someone within the same agency was admitting fault gave us cautious hope.

Naturally, we allowed him to try.

After all, all we've ever wanted was a home.

Adam only came the one time.

We didn't hear from him again for seven months-but we didn't push for updates during that time.

When we finally reached out in July 2023, Adam acted like he didn't know who we were.

When reminded, he apologized and promised to meet with us the next day at 11 a.m.

He apologized again and rescheduled for the following day.

He ghosted us again-and that was the last we ever heard from Adam.

It is still unclear what his purpose was, or why he reached out in the first place.

That brief glimmer of honesty-his recognition that we'd been wronged-was snuffed out the moment he vanished.

To this day, we are bewildered as to what his endgame was.

It felt like someone staged an apology only to remind us who holds the power.

False Hope Instance #3 - Stephen Heath's Slippery Support Following Adam's disappearance, we were assigned to Stephen Heath.

At first, Stephen projected professionalism.

He collected our documents, reviewed our file, and told us we would need to wait for the release of HUD vouchers.

He never gave a clear date-even when we told him we'd heard they were expected to be released on October 1st, which was publicly listed on HUD's site.

When we asked directly, Stephen wouldn't confirm it.

He remained vague, his cynical tone growing more noticeable with each visit.

Still, we wanted to believe in him.

At one point, fully convinced he was diligently working on our case, Joshua texted Stephen a message thanking him for his efforts and letting him know his work mattered.

Stephen never replied to the text.

But the next time he saw us, he said something we'll never forget: "Don't thank me yet." At the time, we thought it was humility.

In hindsight, it was ominous.

Stephen would later contradict himself multiple times-about timelines, about program rules, and most critically, about income requirements.

At one point, he told us outright that having no income was a benefit in the program.

But later, under pressure, he'd claim the only reason we weren't housed was because we didn't submit proof of income.

Stephen is someone who tries to play both sides-but, as always, that tactic eventually collapses.

False Hope Instance #4 - The Taylor Alvarez Call and What Followed By the time October 1st came-the expected HUD voucher release date-Stephen still hadn't given us any clear direction.

This revelation was deeply unsettling, so we texted Stephen for clarity.

His response was curt: he said it was now "out of his hands" and gave us a phone number for someone named Taylor.

We assumed Taylor was our new contact with HUD, and for the first time in months, we felt like progress was imminent.

But when we called Taylor, the experience was devastating.

She had no idea who we were.

She claimed to have never heard of our case.

Most shocking of all-she denied knowing what Rapid Rehousing even was.

This was a red flag, especially since her LinkedIn profile clearly listed her as the Senior Director of Homeless Services at New Hope, overseeing Rapid Rehousing, Stability Vouchers, and program outcomes.

When we pointed this out to her, she became angry and dismissive, accusing Janina of "throwing a tantrum" for not getting her way.

The call ended abruptly.

Within an hour of posting about the contradiction on New Hope's Facebook page, our comment was deleted.

We went right back to Stephen.

It had "returned to his hands," whether he liked it or not.

But Stephen didn't handle it well.

Between that phone call and the illegal eviction in January, Stephen showed up at least twice.

But he had never given us a number for Homeless Trust.

Nor had he ever instructed us to call them.

He was doing his best to convince us that we had simply forgotten-but we hadn't.

His behavior was visibly uncomfortable and increasingly awkward.

We didn't challenge him.

It was obvious that Stephen was trying to extract himself from the situation.

He had tried to do so with Taylor-but when that failed, it fell back on him.

We called Homeless Trust right after he left, only to be told by the woman who answered: "The Homeless Trust doesn't deal with the public." We also contacted HUD.

One number led us to a man who claimed not to know what Rapid Rehousing was.

Still, we gave New Hope the benefit of the doubt.

We told ourselves maybe they hadn't checked the right list.

We desperately wanted to believe Stephen had been trying his best, that there was just a miscommunication.

But even confirmation bias has limits.

New Hope had been stringing us along for far too long.

The Illegal Eviction In January 2024, events reached a breaking point.

He pretended to be offering help and made a phone call to Taylor Alvarez right in front of us.

He said New Hope would send a team to assist us.

That team never came.

Days later, Stephen Heath showed up instead.

He began by lamenting that posting "those 5-day notices" made him look bad with the homeless community.

Then, without warning, he pulled out one of those notices and posted it himself, affixing it to a nearby pole.
